x\in I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right).
By combining (
1.5
) and (
1.6
) with equality (
2.14
) in Lemma
2.8
we obtain that

D_{M_{l_{i}}}=0

and

D_{s_{n_{k}}M_{s_{n_{k}}}}=K_{s_{n_{k}}M_{{s_{n_{k}}}}}=0,\ \ s_{n_{k}}>l_{i}.

Since
s_{n_{1}}>s_{n_{2}}>\dots>s_{n_{r}}\geq 0
we find that

\displaystyle n^{(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n-\sum_{i=1}^{k}s_{n_{i}}M_{n_{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k+1}^{s}s_{n_{i}}M_{n_{i}}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n_{k+1}}(m_{i}-1)M_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{n_{k+1}}M_{n_{k+1}}-1\leq M_{n_{k}}.

According to (
1.11
) we have that

\displaystyle n\left|K_{n}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|s_{l_{i}}M_{l_{i}}K_{s_{l_{i}}M_{l_{i}}}\right|

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{i-1}\sum_{k=l_{r}}^{m_{r}}\left|s_{k}M_{k}K_{s_{k}M_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{i-1}\sum_{k=l_{r}}^{m_{r}}\left|M_{k}D_{s_{k}M_{k}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}-I_{2}-I_{3}.

Let
x\in I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right)
and
1\leq s_{l_{i}}\leq m_{l_{i}}-1
. By using Lemma
2.8
we get that

\displaystyle I_{1}=\left|s_{l_{i}}M_{l_{i}}K_{s_{l_{i}}M_{l_{i}}}\right|\geq\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{2\pi}\geq\frac{2M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{9}.

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{k}n_{s}^{2}M_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{k}\left(m_{s}-1\right)^{2}M_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{k}m_{s}^{2}M_{s}^{2}-2\sum_{s=0}^{k}m_{s}M_{s}^{2}+\sum_{s=0}^{k}M_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{k}M_{s+1}^{2}-2\sum_{s=0}^{k}M_{s+1}M_{s}+\sum_{s=0}^{k}M_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{k+1}^{2}+2\sum_{s=0}^{k}M_{s}^{2}-2\sum_{s=0}^{k}M_{s+1}M_{s}-M_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{k+1}^{2}-1.

and

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{k}n_{s}M_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{k}\left(m_{s}-1\right)M_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{k}M_{k}-m_{0}M_{0}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{k+1}-2.

Since
m_{i-1}\leq l_{i}-2
if we use the estimates above, then we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{l_{i}-2}\left|n_{s}M_{s}K_{n_{s}M_{s}}\left(x\right)\right|

(2.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{l_{i}-2}n_{s}M_{s}\frac{\left(n_{s}M_{s}+1\right)}{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(m_{l_{i}-2}-1\right)M_{l_{i}-2}}{2}\sum_{s=0}^{l_{i}-2}\left(n_{s}M_{s}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left(m_{l_{i}-2}-1\right)M_{l_{i}-2}}{2}M_{l_{i}-1}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\left(m_{l_{i}-2}-1\right)M_{l_{i}-2}}{2}l_{i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}-1}^{2}}{2}-\frac{M_{l_{i}-2}M_{l_{i}-1}}{2}+M_{l_{i}-1}l_{i}.

For
I_{3}
we have that

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{l_{i}-2}\left|M_{k}D_{n_{k}M_{k}}\left(x\right)\right|

(2.21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{l_{i}-2}n_{k}M_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{l_{i}-2}\sum_{k=0}^{l_{i}-2}n_{k}M_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{l_{i}-1}M_{l_{i}-2}-2M_{l_{i}-2}.

By combining (
2.2
)-(
2.21
) we have that

\displaystyle n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle I_{1}-I_{2}-I_{3}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{2\pi}+\frac{3}{2}+2M_{l_{i}-2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}-1}M_{l_{i}-2}}{2}-\frac{M_{l_{i}-1}^{2}}{2}-M_{l_{i}-1}l_{i}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{2\pi}-\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{16}-\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{8}+\frac{7}{2}-M_{l_{i}-1}l_{i}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{9}-\frac{3M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{16}+\frac{7}{2}-M_{l_{i}-1}l_{i}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{144}-M_{l_{i}-1}l_{i}.

Suppose that
l_{i}\geq 4
. Then

\displaystyle n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{36}-\frac{M_{l_{i}}}{4}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{36}-\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{64}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{5M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{36\cdot 16}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{l_{i}}^{2}}{144}.

The proof is complete.

Next corollary is simple consequence of Lemma
2.8
:

Corollary 2.9

Let
2<n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
q_{n}=M_{2n}+M_{2n-2}+...+M_{2}+M_{0}.
Then

q_{n-1}\left|K_{q_{n-1}}(x)\right|\geq\frac{M^{2}_{2k}}{144},\text{ \ \ for \ \ }x\in I_{2k+1}\left(e_{2k-1}+e_{l_{2k}}\right),

where
k=0,1,...,n.

2.3
Strong convergence of partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series on martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we investigate some new strong convergence result of of partial sums of Vilenkin-Fourier series:

Theorem 2.10

a) Let
f\in H_{1}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

b) Let
\varphi:\mathbb{N_{+}}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be a nondecreasing
function satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log n}{\varphi_{n}}=+\infty.

(2.22)

Then there exists a function
f\in H_{1},
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n\varphi_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}=\infty.

Proof:

By using (
2.6
) we can conclude that

\frac{1}{n\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}\leq\frac{c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}}{n\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}{\log k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

and the proof of part a) is complete.

Under the condition (
2.22
) there exists an increasing sequence of the positive integers
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\overline{\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}=+\infty

and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{\log^{1/2}M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}<c<\infty.

(2.23)

Let
f=(f^{(n)},\ \ n\in\mathbb{N})
be martingale, defined by

f^{(n)}:=\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

where

a_{k}=r_{\alpha_{k}}D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}=D_{2M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}

and

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{\log^{1/2}M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}.

By the definition of
H_{1}
and Lemma
1.3
, if we apply (
2.23
) we can conclude that
f\in H_{1}.
Moreover,

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\lambda_{k}},\,\,\text{\ \ \ \ \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}},...,2M_{\alpha_{k}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\\
0\text{ },\text{ \thinspace\qquad\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}},...,2M_{\alpha_{k}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.24)

Since

D_{j+M_{\alpha_{k}}}=D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}+\psi_{{}_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}}D_{j},\text{ \hskip 20.00003ptwhen \thinspace\thinspace}j\leq M_{\alpha_{k}},

if we apply (
2.24
) we obtain that

\displaystyle S_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}

(2.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+{\lambda_{k}}\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{j-1}\psi_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+{\lambda_{k}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+{\lambda_{k}}\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}D_{j-M_{\alpha_{k}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}.

In view of (
2.1
) we can write that

\displaystyle\left\|I_{1}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f\right\|_{1}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

(2.26)

By combining (
2.26
) with lower estimate in
1.13
we get that

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|I_{2}\right\|_{1}-\left\|I_{1}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{\lambda_{k}}{L\left({n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right)}-c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c{\lambda_{k}}{v\left({n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right)}-c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

Hence, by applying (
1.14
) we find that

\displaystyle\underset{n\in\mathbf{\mathbb{N}}_{+}}{\sup}\frac{1}{n\varphi_{n}}\underset{k=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq l\leq 2M_{\alpha_{k}}\right\}}{\sum}\left\|S_{l}f\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{2M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq l\leq 2M_{\alpha_{k}}\right\}}{\sum}\left(\frac{v\left(l-M_{\alpha_{k}}\right)\varphi_{2M{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{\log^{1/2}M_{\alpha_{k}}}-c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\varphi_{2M{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{2M_{\alpha_{k}}\log^{1/2}M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{l=1}{\overset{M_{\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}v\left(l\right)-c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\varphi_{2M{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}\log M_{\alpha_{k}}}{\log^{1/2}M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\log^{1/2}M_{\alpha_{k}}}{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Using theorem of Weisz
[
176
]
in the case
p=1
we get that there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{1}<c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

That is,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}S_{k}f\right\|_{1}<c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

(2.27)

This estimate arise an interesting question, if there exists an absolute absolute constant
c,
such that the following strong convergence result

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}<c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}}.

holds true, which is stronger inequality than (
2.27
). In particular, we have negative answer on this question:

Corollary 2.11

There exists a function
f\in H_{1},
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}=\infty.

2.4
Strong convergence of Vilenkin-Fejér means on martingale Hardy spaces

The main result of this section reads:

Theorem 2.12

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

b) Let
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be a nondecreasing
function satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log n}{\varphi_{n}}=+\infty.

(2.28)

Then there exists a function
f\in H_{1/2},
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n\varphi_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}=\infty.

Corollary 2.13

There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2},
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}=\infty.

Proof:

By using
2.1
was proved that there exists an absolute constant
c
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}\leq c\log k\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2},\ \ k=1,2,...

Hence,

\frac{1}{n\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}\leq\frac{c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{n\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}{\log k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

The proof of part a) is complete.

Under the condition (
2.28
) there exists an increasing sequence of the positive integers
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\overline{\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}=+\infty

and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}{\log^{1/2}M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}<c<\infty.

(2.29)

Let
f=(f^{(n)},\ \ n\in\mathbb{N})
be martingale, defined by

f^{(n)}:=\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

where

a_{k}=M_{\alpha_{k}}r_{\alpha_{k}}D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}=M_{\alpha_{k}}(D_{2M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}})

and

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}{\log M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}.

Since

S_{2^{A}}a_{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}a_{k},&\alpha_{k}<A,\\
0,&\alpha_{k}\geq A,\end{array}\right.

(2.30)

\text{supp}(a_{k})=I_{\alpha_{k}},\text{\ }\int_{I_{\alpha_{k}}}a_{k}d\mu=0,\text{\ }\left\|a_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq M^{2}_{\alpha_{k}}=\mu(\text{supp }a_{k})^{-2},

if we apply Lemma
1.3
and (
2.29
) we conclude that
f\in H_{1/2}.

Moreover,

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}M_{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}},\,\,\text{\ \ \ \ \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}},...,2M_{\alpha_{k}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\\
0\text{ },\text{ \thinspace\qquad\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}},...,2M_{\alpha_{k}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.31)

We have that

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{\alpha_{k}}-1}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}S_{j}f

(2.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I+II.

Let
M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq j<2M_{\alpha_{k}}.
Since

D_{j+M_{\alpha_{k}}}=D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}+\psi_{{}_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}}D_{j},\text{ \hskip 20.00003ptwhen \thinspace\thinspace}j\leq M_{\alpha_{k}},

if we apply (
4.32
) we obtain that

\displaystyle S_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}

(2.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+M_{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{j-1}\psi_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+M_{\alpha_{k}}{\lambda_{k}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f+{\lambda_{k}}\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}D_{j-M_{\alpha_{k}}}

According to (
2.33
) concerning
II
we conclude can that

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}{n}S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{\alpha_{k}}}{n}\sum_{j=M_{2\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}D_{j-M_{\alpha_{k}}}

\displaystyle=II_{1}+II_{2}.

We can estimate
II_{2}
as fallows:

\displaystyle\left|II_{2}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{\alpha_{k}}}{n}\left|\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-M_{\alpha_{k}}-1}D_{j}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{k}M_{\alpha_{k}}}{n}{(n-M_{\alpha_{k}})}\left|K_{n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{\lambda_{k}}\left(n-M_{\alpha_{k}}\right)\left|K_{n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right|.

Let
n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{i}}^{m_{i}}M_{k},
where

0\leq l_{1}\leq m_{1}\leq l_{2}-2<l_{2}\leq m_{2}\leq...\leq l_{s}-2<l_{s}\leq m_{s}.

By applying Lemma
2.4
we get that

\displaystyle\left|II_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{c\lambda_{k}\left|\left(n-M_{\alpha_{k}}\right)K_{n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\left(x\right)\right|}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{c\lambda_{k}M_{l_{i}}^{2}},\text{ \ \ for \ \ }x\in I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right).

Hence

\displaystyle\int_{G_{m}}\left|II_{2}\right|^{1/2}d\mu

(2.34)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\int_{I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right)}\left|II_{2}\right|^{1/2}d\mu

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\int_{I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right)}{\lambda_{k}^{1/2}M_{l_{i}}}d\mu

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{c\lambda_{k}^{1/2}\left(s-1\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{c\lambda_{k}^{1/2}v\left(n-M_{\alpha_{k}}\right)}.

In view of (
2.1
), (
2.9
) and (
2.32
) we find that

\displaystyle\left\|I\right\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}}{n}\sigma_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

(2.35)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

and

\displaystyle\left\|II_{1}\right\|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}{n}S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

(2.36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

By combining (
2.34
), (
2.35
) and (
2.36
) we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}-\left\|II_{1}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}-\left\|I\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{c\lambda_{k}^{1/2}v\left({n-M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right)}-c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

By using estimates with the above we can conclude that

\displaystyle\underset{n\in\mathbf{\mathbb{N}}_{+}}{\sup}\frac{1}{n\varphi_{n}}\underset{k=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

(2.37)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq l\leq 2M_{\alpha_{k}}\right\}}{\sum}\left\|\sigma_{l}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq l\leq 2M_{\alpha_{k}}\right\}}{\sum}\left({\lambda_{k}^{1/2}v\left(l-M_{\alpha_{k}}\right)}-c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\lambda_{k}^{1/2}}{M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{l=1}{\overset{M_{\alpha_{k}}}{\sum}}v\left(l\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}}\leq l\leq 2M_{\alpha_{k}}\right\}}{\sum}1

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\lambda_{k}^{1/2}}{M_{\alpha_{k}}\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}}\underset{l=1}{\overset{M_{\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}v\left(l\right)-c

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\frac{\log^{1/2}M_{{\alpha_{k}}}}{\varphi_{2M_{\alpha_{k}}}^{1/2}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

2.5
Convergence of subsequences of Vilenkin-Fejér means on the martingale Hardy spaces

Our main result of this section reads:

Theorem 2.14

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an
absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

b) (sharpness) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\Phi\left(n\right)
be
any nondecreasing function, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty,\text{ \ \ }\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\Phi\left(n_{k}\right)}=\infty.

(2.38)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(n_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Proof:
by using (
1.12
) we obtain that

\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}

is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}.
According to Lemma
1.4
we find that the proof of
Theorem will be complete, if we show that

\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}<c<\infty,

for every
p
-atom
a,
with support
\ I
and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
\sigma_{n_{k}}\left(a\right)=0
when
n_{k}\leq M_{N}.
Therefore, we can
suppose that
n_{k}>M_{N}
.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
we find that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}

(2.39)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{N}^{1/p}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{2}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Without loss the generality we may assume that
i<j
. Let
x\in I_{N}^{i,j}\
and
j<\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle.
Then
x-t\in I_{N}^{i,j}
for
t\in I_{N}
and, according to (
1.9
), we obtain that

\left|K_{M_{l}}\left(x-t\right)\right|=0,\text{ \ for all }\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle\leq\text{ }l\leq\left|n_{k}\right|.

(2.40)

By combining (
2.17
) in Lemma
2.3
with (
2.39
) and (
2.40
), for
x\in I_{N}^{i,j},\text{ \ }0\leq i<j<\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}

(2.41)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{2}\overset{\left|n_{k}\right|}{\underset{l=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{l}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)=0.\text{ }

Let
x\in I_{N}^{i,j},\,
where
\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle\leq j\leq N.
Then, in the view of Lemma
2.4
, we have that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{i}M_{j}}{M_{N}^{2}}.

By using again (
2.39
) we find that

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}

(2.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{N}^{1/p}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n_{k}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{N}^{1/p}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\frac{M_{i}M_{j}}{M_{N}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{i}M_{j}.

By using (
1.1
) we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overset{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle+\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle+\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,N}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overset{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{i}M_{j}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,j}}\left|M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{i}M_{j}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle+\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{i,N}}\left|M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}M_{i}M_{N}\right|^{p}d\mu

Hence, by combining (
2.39
-
2.42
) we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}\left|\sigma_{n_{k}}a\left(x\right)\right|}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}\overset{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\frac{\left(M_{i}M_{j}\right)^{p}}{M_{j}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\frac{\left(M_{i}M_{j}\right)^{p}}{M_{j}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}\underset{i=0}{\sum}\frac{\left(M_{i}M_{N}\right)^{p}}{M_{N}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}\overset{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}^{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{j}^{1-p}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}M_{i}^{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{j=i+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{j}^{1-p}}

\displaystyle+c_{p}\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{i}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{p}\frac{1}{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-p}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{i=\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{i}^{1-2p}}+c_{p}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

The proof of the a) part is complete.

b) Let

\left\{n_{k}:k\geq 0\right\}
be a sequence of positive numbers,
satisfying condition (
2.38
). Then

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}}{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}}=\infty.

(2.43)

Under condition (
2.43
) there exists a sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}
such
that
\alpha_{0}\geq 3
and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1-2p\right)/2}\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1-2p\right)/2}}<c<\infty.

(2.44)

Let

f^{\left(n\right)}=\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }\left|\alpha_{k}\right|<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}

and

a_{k}=\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right).

B applying Lemma
1.3
we can conclude that
f\in H_{p}.

It is evident that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right),\,\,\text{ }\\
\text{if \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k=0,1,2...,\\
0\text{ },\text{ \thinspace\qquad\thinspace\ \ \ \ \thinspace\ \ \ \ \ }\\
\text{\ if \thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,\text{ ~}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.45)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}S_{j}f

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I+II.

Let
M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<j\leq\alpha_{k}.
Then, by
applying (
2.45
) we get that

S_{j}f=S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f+M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right).

(2.46)

By using (
2.46
) we can rewrite
II
as

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}^{\alpha_{k}}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle II_{1}+II_{2}.

If we combine (
2.1
) and (
2.9
) it is easy to show that

\displaystyle\left\|II_{1}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)^{p}\left\|S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)^{p}\left\|S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}<\infty.

and

\displaystyle\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)^{p}\left\|\sigma_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)^{p}\left\|\sigma_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}<\infty.

Let
x\in

I_{{}_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}}^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle-1,\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}.
Under
condition (
2.38
) we can conclude that
\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\neq\left|\alpha_{k}\right|
and
\left\langle\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right\rangle=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle.
Since

D_{j+M_{n}}=D_{M_{n}}+\psi_{M_{n}}D_{j}=D_{M_{n}}+r_{n}D_{j},\text{ when }\,\,j<M_{n}

(2.47)

if we apply estimate Lemma
2.7
for
II_{2}
we obtain
that

\displaystyle\left|II_{2}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\left(D_{j+M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{\alpha_{k}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\left|\psi_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p-1}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2p-1}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+2\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left(\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+2\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)^{p}\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|IV_{2}\right|\geq c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)/2}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{\left(1/p+2\right)/2}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2-p}M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/2+p}\mu\left\{I_{{}_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}}^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle-1,\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\right\}}{\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2-p}}{M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/2-p}\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}.

Hence, for large
k
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\left\|II_{1}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|II_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/2-p}}{2M_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/2-p}\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Corollary 2.15

Let
0<p<1/2,
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists
an absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }k\in\mathbb{N}

if and only if when

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)<c<\infty.

As a application we also obtain the previous mentioned result by Weisz
[
174
]
,
[
175
]
(Theorem W).

Corollary 2.16

Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists
an absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma_{M_{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}.

On the other hand, the following unexpected result is true:

Corollary 2.17

a)Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma_{M_{n}+1}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}M^{1/p-2}_{n}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}.

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\Phi\left(n\right)
be any nondecreasing function, such that

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi\left(k\right)}=\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{M_{k}+1}f}{\Phi\left(k\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Remark 2.18

From Corollary
2.16
we obtain that
\sigma_{M_{n}}f
are bounded from
H_{p}
to
H_{p}
, but from Corollary
2.17
we conclude that
\sigma_{M_{n}+1}f
are not bounded from
H_{p}
to
H_{p}
. The main reason is that Fourier coefficients of martingale
f\in H_{p}
are not uniformly bounded (for details see e.g.
[
145
]
).

We also state the following corollary which shows difference of the rate of divergence across to different subsequences.

Corollary 2.19

a)Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n}+M_{[n/2]}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}\left(M_{n}/M_{[n/2]}\right)^{1/p-2}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }n\in\mathbb{N},

where
[n/2]
denotes integer part of
n/2
.

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\Phi\left(n\right)
be any non-decreasing function, such that

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{{\left(M_{k}/M_{[k/2]}\right)}^{1/p-2}}{\Phi\left(k\right)}=\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{M_{k}+/M_{[k/2]}}f}{\Phi\left(k\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

In the next corollary we state Corollaries
2.17
and
2.19
for Walsh system only to clearly see difference of divergence rates for the various subsequences:

Corollary 2.20

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma^{w}_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}2^{(1/p-2)n}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}

(2.48)

and

\text{ }\left\|\sigma^{w}_{2^{n}+2^{[n/2]}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq c_{p}2^{\frac{(1/p-2)n}{2}}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ \ }n\in\mathbb{N},

(2.49)

where
[n/2]
denotes integer part of
n/2
.

b) The rates
2^{(1/p-2)n}
and
2^{\frac{(1/p-2)n}{2}}
in inequalities (
2.48
) and (
2.49
) are sharp in the same sense as in Theorem
2.14
.

2.6
necessary and sufficient condition for the norm convergence of subsequences of partial sums in terms of modulus of continuity

Theorem 2.21

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
,
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}d\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=o\left(\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.50)

Then

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.51)

b) Let
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty.
Then there exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
for which

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=O\left(\frac{M_{\left\langle n_{k}\right\rangle}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

(2.52)

and

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.53)

Proof:

Let
0<p<1/2,\ f\in H_{p}
and
M_{k}<n\leq M_{k+1}.
By applying part a) of Theorem
2.14
we can conclude that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

\leq\left\|\sigma_{n}f-\sigma_{n}S_{M_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{k}}f-S_{M_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

=\left\|\sigma_{n}\left(S_{M_{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|S_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{k}}f-S_{M_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

\leq c_{p}\left(\frac{M_{\left|n\right|}^{1-2p}}{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{1-2p}}+1\right)\omega_{H_{p}}^{p}\left(1/M_{n},f\right)+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{k}}f-S_{M_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

By simple calculation we get that

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

(2.54)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{N}}S_{k}S_{M_{N}}f+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=M_{N}+1}^{n}S_{k}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{N}}S_{k}f+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=M_{N}+1}^{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{M_{N}}S_{k}f+\frac{n-M_{N}}{n}S_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{N}}{n}\sigma_{M_{N}}f-\frac{M_{N}}{n}S_{M_{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{N}}{n}\left(S_{M_{N}}\sigma_{M_{N}}f-S_{M_{N}}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{N}}{n}S_{M_{N}}\left(\sigma_{M_{N}}f-f\right).

Let
p>0.
By combining (
2.1
) and (
2.9
) we can conclude that

\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{M_{k}}f-S_{M_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

(2.55)

\leq\frac{2^{M_{k}}}{n^{p}}\left\|S_{M_{k}}\left(\sigma_{M_{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|\sigma_{M_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty\text{.}

On the other hand, under the condition (
2.50
) we also get that

c_{p}\left(\frac{M_{\left|n\right|}^{1-2p}}{M_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}^{1-2p}}+1\right)\omega_{H_{p}}^{p}\left(1/M_{n},f\right)\rightarrow 0

(2.56)

by combining (
2.55
) and (
2.56
) we complete the proof of theorem.

Now, prove second part of theorem. Since
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty,
we obtain that, for
0<p<1/2,

\frac{M_{\left<n_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left|n_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}=\frac{1}{\left(m_{\left<n_{k}\right>}\ldots m_{\left|n_{k}\right|-1}\right)^{1/p-2}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{\rho\left(n_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}}\rightarrow 0.

It follows that there exists
\{\alpha_{k}:k\geq 1\}\subset\{n_{k}:k\geq 1\}
such that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(\alpha_{k}\right)=\infty

and

\frac{M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\leq\left(\frac{M_{\left<\alpha_{k-1}\right>}^{1/p-2}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k-1}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right)^{2}\hskip 10.00002pt\text{for all}\hskip 10.00002ptk\in\mathbb{N}.

(2.57)

By using (
2.57
) we get that

\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\leq\left(\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{k-1}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k-1}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right)^{2}\leq\ldots\leq\left(\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{0}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{0}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right)^{k+1}\leq\frac{1}{2^{(k+1)\left(\left|\alpha_{0}\right|-\left<\alpha_{0}\right>\right)(1/p-2)}}

and

\underset{k=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\left(\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-2}}\right)^{p}\leq\underset{k=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{(k+1)\left(\left|\alpha_{0}\right|-\left<\alpha_{0}\right>\right)(1-2p)}}<c<\infty.

(2.58)

We set
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
where

f^{\left(n\right)}=\sum_{\left\{i:\text{ }\left|\alpha_{i}\right|<n\right\}}\frac{\lambda{M_{\left<\alpha_{i}\right>}^{\left(1/p-2\right)}}}{{M_{\left|\alpha_{i}\right|}^{\left(1/p-2\right)}}}a_{i}^{\left(p\right)},

a_{k}^{(p)}:=\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)

and

\lambda=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}m_{k}.

Since
a_{i}^{(p)}(x)
is
p
-atom if we apply Lemma
1.3
and (
2.58
) we conclude that
f\in H_{p}.
On the other hand, if we apply Remark
1.6
we find that

f-S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{{}_{n}}\right|}}f=\left(0,...,0,\underset{i=n}{\overset{n+s}{\sum}}\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{i}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{\left|\alpha_{i}\right|}^{1/p-2}}a_{i}^{\left(p\right)},...\right),\text{ \ }s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

is martingale. By combining (
2.57
) and Lemma
1.3
we get that

\omega_{H_{p}}(1/M_{\left|\alpha_{{}_{n}}\right|},f)

\leq\sum\limits_{i=n}^{\infty}\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{i}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{{M_{\left|\alpha_{i}\right|}^{1/p-2}}}\leq\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{n}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{{M_{\left|\alpha_{n}\right|}^{1/p-2}}}\right)^{i}=O\left(\frac{{M_{\left<\alpha_{n}\right>}^{1/p-2}}}{{M_{\left|\alpha_{{}_{n}}\right|}^{1/p-2}}}\right),\ \ \text{as}\ \ n\rightarrow\infty.

It is easy to show that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2},&\text{\thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k=0,1,...\\
0\,,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}\left\{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.59)

Let
M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<j<\alpha_{k}.
By using (
2.59
) we get that

\displaystyle S_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f+\sum_{v=M{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v)w_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f+M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)

Hence,

\sigma_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}f=\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}S_{j}f

(2.60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\sigma_{{}_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f+\frac{\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f}{\alpha_{k}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2)}}{\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}\left(D_{{}_{j}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I+II+III.

Since

D_{j+M_{n}}=D_{M_{n}}+\psi_{M_{n}}D_{j},\text{ \thinspace when \thinspace}j<M_{n+1}

we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|III\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}{\alpha_{k}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-M_{{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\left(D_{j+M_{{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}-D_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\right|

(2.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}M_{\left<n_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}{\alpha_{k}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{j}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}}{\alpha_{k}}\left(\alpha_{k}-M{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle cM_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1/p-2}\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right|.

By combining (
2.60
) and (
2.61
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}=\|I+II+III-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|III+\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\sigma_{{}_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f+\frac{\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f}{\alpha_{k}}-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|III+\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\left(\sigma_{{}_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\right)+\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\left(S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f-f\right)\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|III\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\left(\frac{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}\|\sigma_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}\|S_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|III\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\|\sigma_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}-\|S_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}.

If we combine (
2.1
) and (
2.9
) it is easy to show that

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty,

\displaystyle\|S_{M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty,

Hence, for sufficiently large
k,
we can write that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|III\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\geq\frac{M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{1-2p}}{2}\|\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}

Let
x\in E_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}.
By using Lemma

2.7
we have that

\displaystyle\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|\geq cM_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mu\left(E_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>}\right)\geq c/M_{\left<\alpha_{k}\right>},

and

cM_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{2p}\mu\left\{x\in G:\left(\alpha_{k}-M_{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-M_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|\geq cM_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{2p}\right\}\geq cM_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}^{2p-1}.

Hence,

\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as}\,\,\,k\rightarrow\infty

and Theorem is proved.

On the other hand, the following unexpected new result is also obtained:

Corollary 2.22

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{n_{k}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{M_{n_{k}}^{1/p-2}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n_{k}}+1}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

b) Let
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty.
Then there exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
for which

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{M_{n_{k}}^{1/p-2}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\sigma_{{M_{n_{k}}+1}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

Corollary 2.23

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{n_{k}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{{M_{[n_{k}/2]}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{n_{k}}^{1/p-2}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n_{k}}+M_{[n_{k}/2]}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

b) Let
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\rho\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty.
Then there exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}(G_{m})

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
for which

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=O\left(\frac{{M_{[n_{k}/2]}^{1/p-2}}}{M_{n_{k}}^{1/p-2}}\right),

and

\left\|\sigma_{M_{n_{k}}+M_{[n_{k}/2]}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

In the next corollary we state theorem for Walsh system only to clearly see difference of divergence rates for the various subsequences:

Corollary 2.24

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/2^{n},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{n(1/p-2)}}\right),\text{ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma^{w}_{2^{n}+1}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

b) There exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
for which

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/2^{n},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{n(1/p-2)}}\right),

and

\left\|\sigma^{w}_{2^{n}+1}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

Corollary 2.25

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/2^{n},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{n(1/p-2)/2}}\right),\text{ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma^{w}_{2^{n}+2^{[n/2]}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

where
[n/2]
denotes integer part of
n/2
.

b) There exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}(G_{m})

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
for which

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(1/M_{n},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{n(1/p-2)/2}}\right)

and

\left\|\sigma^{w}_{2^{n}+2^{[n/2]}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}n\rightarrow\infty.

3
T
means of Vilenkin-Fourier series on martingale Hardy spaces

3.1
Some classical results on
T
means of Vilenkin-Fourier series

It is well-known in the literature that the so-called
T
means are generalizations of the Fejér, Cesàro and logarithmic means. The
T
summation is a general summability method. Therefore it is of prior
interest to study the behavior of operators related to Nörlund means of
Fourier series with respect to orthonormal systems.

Since
T
means are inverse of Nörlund means we state some interesting results concerning Nörlund summability, which has high influence on the new results for
T
means of Vilenkin-Fourier series.

In
[
47
]
Goginava investigated the behavior of Cesàro means of Walsh-Fourier series in detail. In the two-dimensional case approximation properties of Nörlund and Cesàro means were considered by Nagy (see
[
83
]
,
[
84
]
and
[
86
]
). The maximal operator
\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}

\left(0<\alpha<1\right)
of the
\left(C,\alpha\right)
means of Vilenkin systems was investigated by Weisz
[
179
]
. In this paper Weisz proved that
\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}
is bounded from the martingale space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
for
p>1/\left(1+\alpha\right).
Goginava
[
45
]
gave a counterexample which shows that boundedness does not hold for
0<p\leq 1/\left(1+\alpha\right).
Weisz and Simon
[
123
]
showed that the maximal operator
\sigma^{\alpha,\ast}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
to the space
weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
.

Strong convergence theorems and boundedness of weighted maximal operators of of the
\left(C,\alpha\right)
means of
Vilenkin systems on the Hardy spaces, when
0<p\leq 1/(1+\alpha)
were considered by Blahota, Tephnadze
[
15
]
and Blahota, Tephnadze, Toledo
[
16
]
. Summability of some general methods were considered by Blahota, Nagy and Tephnadze
[
12
]
, Weisz,

In Persson,Tephnadze and Wall
[
104
]
(see also
[
145
]
) considered
maximal operator of the Nörlund summation method (see (
1.17
)). In particular, the maximal operator
t^{\ast}
of the
summability method (
1.17
) with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

Moreover,for any
0<p<1/2
and non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}

satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{0}}{Q_{n}}\geq\frac{c}{n},\text{ \ \ }\left(c>0\right),

(3.1)

there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

In Persson, Tephnadze and Wall
[
106
]
was proved that if
0<p<1/2
and the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing, then the maximal operator

\widetilde{t}_{p,1}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}

is bounded from the Hardy martingale space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

On the other hand, according the fact that Fejér means are examples of Nörlund means with
non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
we
immediately obtain that the
asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of weights

\left\{1/\left(k+1\right)^{1/p-2}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

in Nörlund means can not be improved.

Let the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing. Then the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{t}_{1}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1/2}.

On the other hand, according the fact that Fejér means are examples of Nörlund means with non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
we
immediately obtain that the
asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of weights

\left\{1/\log^{2}\left(n+1\right):n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

in Nörlund means can not be improved.

Persson, Tephnade and Wall
[
104
]
proved that for all Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
there
exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

It follows that for any
0<p<1/2\ \
and Nörlund means
t_{n}
with
non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
the maximal
operator
t^{\ast}
is not bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}

to the space
weak-L_{p},
that is there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},

such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Persson, Tephnade and
Wall
[
104
]
find necessary condition for the Nörlund means with
non-increasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
, when
1/2\leq p<1.
In particular, for
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),

0<\alpha\leq 1,
and non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}

satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}=c>0,\text{ }0<\alpha\leq 1,

(3.2)

there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

(3.3)

Moreover, for any non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}=\infty,\text{ \ \ \ }\left(0<\alpha\leq 1\right),

(3.4)

there exists an martingale
f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)},
such
that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}=\infty.

(3.5)

It follows that for any
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right),

0<\alpha\leq 1
and non-increasing
sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying the condition (
3.2
). Then there exists a
martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Moreover, if
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a non-increasing sequence satisfying the condition (
3.4
), then there exists an martingale
f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
such
that

\left\|t^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}=\infty.

In
[
80
]
it was proved that the maximal operator
t^{\ast}
of the Nörlund summability method with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\},
satisfying the condition

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{\alpha}}\right),\text{ \ \ when \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty

(3.6)

and

q_{n}-q_{n+1}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2-\alpha}}\right),\text{ \ \ when \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

(3.7)

is bounded from the
Hardy space
H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
to the space
weak-L_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)},
for
0<\alpha\leq 1.

Moreover, for
0<\alpha\leq 1
and non-increasing
sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying the conditions

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{\alpha}}{Q_{n}}\geq c_{\alpha}>0

(3.8)

and

\left|q_{n}-q_{n+1}\right|\geq c_{\alpha}n^{\alpha-2},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}\text{.}

(3.9)

there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}
such
that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{{1/\left(1+\alpha\right)}}=\infty.

In
[
104
]
(see also
[
14
]
) was proved that if
f\in H_{p},
where
0<p\,<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
for some
0<\alpha\leq 1,
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers satisfying conditions (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{t}_{p,\alpha}^{\ast}:=\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

Moreover, let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence, satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty,

(3.10)

then there exists Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
satisfying the conditions (
3.8
) and (
3.9
) such
that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\frac{t_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}f_{k}}{\Phi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}}\right\|_{weal-L_{p}}}{\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}}}=\infty.

It follows that if
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
and
f\in H_{p},
then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p,\alpha},

depending only on
p
and
\alpha
, such that

\left\|t_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p,\alpha}\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1-\alpha}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}},\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

On the other hand, let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition (
3.10
), then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{t_{n}f}{\Phi_{n}}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Moreover, let
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be any non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition (
3.10
), then the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\Phi_{n}}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

In
[
17
]
(see also
[
14
]
) was proved if
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)},
where
0<\alpha\leq 1

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers
satisfying the conditions (
3.6
) and (

), then there exists an absolute
constant
c_{\alpha}
depending only on
\alpha
such that the maximal
operator

\overset{\sim}{t}_{\alpha}^{\ast}:=\frac{\left|t_{n}f\right|}{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{1/(1+\alpha)}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{1/(1+\alpha)}.

Moreover, if
\left\{\Phi_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be any
non-decreasing sequence satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log^{1+\alpha}\left(n+1\right)}{\Phi_{n}}=+\infty,

(3.11)

then there exists Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}

) and (

) such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left\|\sup_{n}\left|\frac{t_{n}f_{k}}{\Phi_{n}}\right|\right\|_{1/(1+\alpha)}}{\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/(1+\alpha)}}}=\infty.

In Persson, Tephnadze and Wall
[
106
]
was proved that if
0<p<1/2
,
f\in H_{p}
and the sequence

c_{p}
depending only on

such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

On the other hand, according the fact that Fejér means are examples of Nörlund means with
non-decreasing sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
we
immediately obtain that
the asymptotic behaviour of the sequence of weights

\left\{1/k^{2-2p}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

in Nörlund means can not be improved.

In Persson, Tephnadze and Wall
[
106
]
was proved if
f\in H_{1/2}
and the sequence
\left\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be non-decreasing satisfying condition (
3.13
), then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|t_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}.

In Blahota and Tephnadze
[
14
]
was investigated Nörlund means with non-increasing sequence

in the case
0<p<1/\left(1+\alpha\right)
where

. In particular, if
f\in H_{p},

In Blahota, Persson and Tephnadze
[

]
was proved that if
f\in H_{1/(1+\alpha)}
where

and

In
[

]
we investigate the maximal operator
T^{\ast}
of the summability method (

)
with non-increasing sequence

is bounded from the Hardy space

to the space

Moreover, for any

and non-increasing sequence

\frac{q_{n+1}}{Q_{n+2}}\geq\frac{c}{n},\text{ \ \ }\left(c\geq 1\right).

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|T_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

We also proved that the maximal operator
T^{\ast}
of the summability method (
1.19
)
with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

Moreover, for any
0<p<1/2
and non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|T_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

In
[
166
]
we proved that for
0<p\leq 1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
the maximal operator

\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

(3.12)

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

On the other hand if
0<p\leq 1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers,satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ \ \ as \ \ }\ n\rightarrow\infty.

(3.13)

,
then the maximal operator

\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

(3.14)

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Since the maximal operator

\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}
and the rate of denominator
\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}
is in the sense sharp and Fejer means is example of
T
means non-decreasing sequence we obtain that this weights are also sharp in (
3.12
) and (
3.14
).

In
[
166
]
we also investigate strong convergence of

means with respect to Vilenkin systems. In particular, if

and

such that the inequality
