holds.

Moreover, if
f\in H_{1/2}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing
numbers, satisfying the condition (
3.13
), then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that the inequality

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

holds.

3.2
Auxiliary lemmas

Lemma 3.1

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

\displaystyle Q_{n}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}q_{j}=\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j+q_{n-1}{(n-1)}

(3.15)

F_{n}=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)jK_{j}+q_{n-1}(n-1)K_{n-1}\right).

(3.16)

t_{n}=\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j\sigma_{j}+q_{n-1}(n-1)\sigma_{n-1}\right).

(3.17)

Proof:

If we invoke Abel transformation we immediately get identities (
3.15
), (
3.16
) and (
3.17
). The proof is complete.

Lemma 3.2

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers, or non-decreasing function satisfying condition (
3.13
). Then

\|F_{n}\|_{1}<c<\infty.

(3.18)

Proof:

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers. By combining (
1.12
) with (
3.15
) and (
3.17
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\|T_{n}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\|\sigma_{j}\|_{1}+q_{n-1}(n-1)\|\sigma_{n-1}\|_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j+q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)\leq c<\infty.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence non-decreasing function satisfying condition (
3.13
). Then By using again (
1.12
) with (
3.15
) and (
3.17
) we find that

\displaystyle\|T_{n}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\|\sigma_{j}\|_{1}+q_{n-1}(n-1)\|\sigma_{n-1}\|_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j+1}-q_{j}\right)j+q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\left(2q_{n-1}(n-1)-\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j+q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c}{Q_{n}}(2q_{n-1}(n-1)-Q_{n})\leq c<\infty.

The proof is complete.

Lemma 3.3

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers and
n>M_{N}
. Then

\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|\right\},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof:

Since sequence is non-increasing number we get that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(q_{M_{N}}+\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|+q_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(q_{M_{N}}+\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)+q_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2q_{M_{N}}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{2q_{M_{N}}}{Q_{M_{N}+1}}\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}}.

If we apply (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) in Lemma
3.1
and Abel transformation we immediately get that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(q_{M_{n}}\sigma_{{M_{n}-1}}+\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)\sigma_{j}+q_{n-1}\sigma_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(q_{M_{n}}+\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|+q_{n-1}\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{M_{N}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|.

The proof is complete.

Lemma 3.4

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\ \ k=0,\dots,N-1,\ \ l=k+1,\dots,N

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers. Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{M^{2}_{N}}.

Here
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
0\leq k<l\leq N-1
and
t\in I_{N}.
First, we observe that
x-t\in

I_{N}^{k,l}.
Next, we apply equality
1.9
and Lemmas
3.4
to obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(3.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{M_{N}}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{M_{N}}\int_{I_{N}}\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{M^{2}_{N}}

and the first estimate is proved.

Now, let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}
. Since
x-t\in I_{N}^{k,N}
for
t\in I_{N},
by
combining (
1.6
) and (
1.7
) we have that

\left|D_{i}\left(x-t\right)\right|\leq M_{k}

and

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(3.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}q_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|D_{i}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\overset{\left|n\right|-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}q_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

According to (
3.19
) and (
3.20
) the proof is complete.

Lemma 3.5

Let
n>M_{N}
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers, satisfying condition (
3.43
). Then

\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{c}{n}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}(x)\right|\right\},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof:

Since sequence is non-increasing number we get that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|+q_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)+q_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{M_{N}}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{n}.

If we apply (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) in Lemma
3.1
we immediately get that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|+q_{n-1}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}(x)\right|.

The proof is complete.

Lemma 3.6

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\ \ k=0,\dots,N-2,\ \ l=k+1,\dots,N-1

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers,
satisfying condition (
3.43
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{nM_{N}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

Here
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},
for
0\leq k<l\leq N-1
and
t\in I_{N}.
First, we observe that
x-t\in

I_{N}^{k,l}.
Next, we apply equality
1.9
and Lemma
3.5
to obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(3.21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{n}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{n}\int_{I_{N}}\overset{l}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{nM_{N}}

and the first estimate is proved.

Now, let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}
. Since
x-t\in I_{N}^{k,N}
for
t\in I_{N},
by
combining again equality (
1.9
) and Lemma
3.5
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

(3.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{n}\underset{i=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{M_{i}}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{n}\overset{\left|n\right|-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}M_{i}\int_{I_{N}}M_{k}d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

By combining (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) we complete the proof.

Lemma 3.7

Let
n\geq M_{N},\ \ x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\ \ k=0,\dots,N-1,\ \ l=k+1,\dots,N
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-increasing sequence, satisfying condition (
3.43
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Since
n\geq M_{N}
if we apply Lemma
3.6
we immediately get the proof.

Lemma 3.8

Let
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-decreasing numbers satisfying (
3.13
).
Then

\left|F_{n}\right|\leq\frac{c}{n}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{j}\left|K_{M_{j}}\right|\right\},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof:

Since sequence
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be non-decreasing. If we apply condition (
3.13
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|+q_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=0}{\sum}}\left(q_{j+1}-q_{j}\right)+q_{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2q_{n-1}-q_{0}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{n}.

If we apply (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) in Lemma
3.1
we immediately get that

\displaystyle\left|F_{n}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|+q_{0}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)+q_{0}\right)\right)\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|\leq\frac{c}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{\left|n\right|}M_{i}\left|K_{M_{i}}\right|.

The proof is complete.

Lemma 3.9

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\ \ k=0,\dots,N-2,\ \ l=k+1,\dots,N-1

and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of non-decreasing numbers,
satisfying condition (
3.13
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{nM_{N}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N},

k=0,\dots,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}.

Here
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
The proof is quite analogously to Lemma
3.6
. So we leave out the details.

Lemma 3.10

Let
n\geq M_{N},\ \ x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\ \ k=0,\dots,N-1,\ \ l=k+1,\dots,N
and
\{q_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
be a sequence of
non-decreasing sequence, satisfying condition (
3.13
). Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}}{M_{N}^{2}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Since
n\geq M_{N}
if we apply Lemma
3.9
we immediately get the proof.

3.3
On the maximal operators of
T
means with respect to Vilenkin systems on the martingale Hardy spaces

First we state our main result concerning the maximal operator of the
summation method (
1.19
), which we also show is in a sense sharp.

Theorem 3.11

a) The maximal operator
T^{\ast}
of the summability method (
1.19
)
with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
is bounded from the Hardy
space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

The statement in a) is sharp in the following sense:

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a non-increasing sequence, satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{n+1}}{Q_{n+2}}\geq\frac{c}{n},\text{ \ \ }\left(c\geq 1\right).

(3.23)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|T_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Proof:
a). Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be non-increasing. By combining (
3.15
) with (
3.16
) and using Abel transformation we get that

\displaystyle\left|T_{n}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}q_{j}S_{j}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|+q_{n-1}(n-1)\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j+q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)\sigma^{\ast}f\leq\sigma^{\ast}f

so that

T^{\ast}f\leq\sigma^{\ast}f.

(3.24)

If we apply (
3.24
), according that
\sigma^{*}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2},
we can conclude that the maximal operators
T^{\ast}
of all
T
means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
are bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.
The proof of part a) of Theorem 1 is complete.

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be an increasing sequence of positive integers such that:

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}1/\alpha_{k}^{p}<\infty,

(3.25)

\lambda\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{M_{\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{\eta}}<\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k}},

(3.26)

\frac{32\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}<\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{\alpha_{k}},

(3.27)

where
\lambda=\sup_{n}m_{n}.

We note that such an increasing sequence

\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

which satisfies conditions (
3.25
), (
3.26
) and (
3.27
) can be constructed.

Let

f^{\left(A\right)}=\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }\lambda_{k}<A\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

(3.28)

where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\lambda}{\alpha_{k}}\ \ \ \ and\ \ \ \ a_{k}=\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\lambda}\left(D_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}}\right).

By using Lemma
1.3
, it is easy to show that the martingale
f\in H_{1/2}.
Moreover, it is easy to show that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}},&\text{if \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}},...,M_{\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k=0,1,2...,\\
0,&\text{if \ \thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{M_{\alpha_{k}},...,M_{\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

(3.29)

We can write

T_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}f=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{\alpha_{k}}}q_{j}S_{j}f+\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}f:=I+II.

(3.30)

Let
M_{\alpha_{s}}\leq

j\leq M_{\alpha_{s}+1},
where
s=0,...,k-1.

Moreover,

\left|D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{s}}}}\right|\leq j-M_{{}_{\alpha_{s}}}\leq\lambda M_{{}_{\alpha_{s}}},\text{ \ \ }\left(s\in\mathbb{N}\right)

so that, according to (
1.3
) and (
3.29
), we have that

\displaystyle\left|S_{j}f\right|=\left|\sum_{v=0}^{M_{\alpha_{s-1}+1}-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}+\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{s}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}\right|

(3.31)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{\eta}}}^{M_{\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\frac{M_{\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}}\psi_{v}\right|+\frac{M_{\alpha_{s}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{s}}\left|\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{s}}}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}\frac{M_{\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}}\left(D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)\right|+\frac{M_{\alpha_{s}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{s}}\left|\left(D_{j}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{s}}}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}\frac{M_{\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{\eta}}+\frac{\lambda M_{\alpha_{s}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{s}}\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{s-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{s-1}}+\frac{\lambda M_{\alpha_{s}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{s}}\leq\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}.

Let
M_{\alpha_{s-1}+1}+1\leq

j\leq M_{\alpha_{s}},
where
s=1,...,k.

Analogously to (
2.25
) we can prove that

\displaystyle\left|S_{j}f\right|=\left|\sum_{v=0}^{M_{\alpha_{s-1}+1}-1}\widehat{f}(v)\psi_{v}\right|=\left|\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}\sum_{v=M_{\alpha_{\eta}}}^{M_{\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\frac{M_{\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}}\psi_{v}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}\frac{M_{\alpha_{\eta}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{\eta}}\left(D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{\eta}+1}}}-D_{M_{{}_{\alpha_{\eta}}}}\right)\right|\leq\frac{2\lambda M_{\alpha_{s-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{s-1}}\leq\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}.

Hence,

\left|I\right|\leq\frac{1}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{\alpha_{k}}}q_{j}\left|S_{j}f\right|\leq\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}\frac{1}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\sum_{j=0}^{M_{\alpha_{k}}}q_{j}\leq\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}.

(3.32)

If we now apply (
3.29
) and (
3.31
) we get that

\displaystyle\left|II\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\left|\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}}\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}+S_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}f\right|

(3.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\left|\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}}\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}+S_{M_{\alpha_{k-1}+1}}f\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\left(\left|\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}}\psi_{M_{\alpha_{k}}}\right|-\left|S_{M_{\alpha_{k-1}+2}}f\right|\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\left(\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{\alpha_{k}}-\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}}.

Without lost the generality we may assume that
c=1
in (
3.23
). By
combining (
3.32
) and (
3.33
) we get

\displaystyle\left|T_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}f\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|II\right|-\left|I\right|\geq\frac{q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+1}}{Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}}\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-1}}{4\alpha_{k}}-\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}

(3.34)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{4\alpha_{k}}-\frac{4\lambda M_{\alpha_{k-1}}^{1/p}}{\alpha_{k-1}}\geq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}}.

On the other hand,

\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|T_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}}\right\}=\mu\left(G_{m}\right)=1.

(3.35)

Let
0<p<1/2.
Then

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}}\cdot\left(\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|T_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}f\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}}\right\}\right)^{1/p}

(3.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{16\alpha_{k}}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

A number of special cases of our results are of particular interest and give both well-known and new information. We just give the following examples of such
T
means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}:

Corollary 3.12

The maximal operators
U^{\alpha,*}
,
V^{\alpha,*}
and
R^{*}
are bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}
but are not bounded from
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p},

when
0<p<1/2.

Proof:

Since
R_{n},U_{n}^{\alpha}\text{ and }V_{n}^{\alpha}
are the
T
means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
then the proof of this corollary is direct consequence of Theorem
3.11
.

Corollary 3.13

Let
f\in L_{1}
and
T_{n}
be the
T
means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
. Then

\ T_{n}f\rightarrow f,\text{ \ \ a.e., \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof:

According to part a) of Theorem
3.11
and Lemma
1.5
we also have weak
(1,1)
type inequality and by well-known density argument due to Marcinkiewicz and Zygmund
[
192
]
(see Lemma
1.1
) we have
T_{n}f\rightarrow f,
a.e., for all
f\in L_{1}.
Which follows proof of Corollary
3.13
.

Corollary 3.14

Let
f\in L_{1}
. Then

\displaystyle R_{n}f

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle f,\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

\displaystyle U_{n}^{\alpha}f

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle f,\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty,\text{\ \ \ }

\displaystyle V_{n}^{\alpha}f

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle f,\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty,\text{\ \ \ }

Proof:

Since
R_{n},U_{n}^{\alpha}\text{ and }V_{n}^{\alpha}
are the
T
means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
then the proof of this corollary is direct consequence of Corollary
3.13
.

Our next main result reads:

Theorem 3.15

a) The maximal operator
T^{\ast}
of the summability method (
1.19
)
with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

(3.37)

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

b) Let
0<p<1/2.
For any non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|T_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

Proof:

Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be
non-decreasing. By combining (
3.15
) with (
3.16
) in Lemma
3.1
and using Abel transformation we get that

\displaystyle\left|T_{n}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}q_{j}S_{j}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|+q_{n-1}(n-1)\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}-\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j-q_{n-1}(n-1)+2q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)\sigma^{\ast}f

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(2q_{n-1}(n-1)-Q_{n}\right)\sigma^{\ast}f\leq c\sigma^{\ast}f

so that

T^{\ast}f\leq c\sigma^{\ast}f.

(3.38)

If we apply (
3.38
), according that
\sigma^{*}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2},
we can conclude that the maximal operators
T^{\ast}
of all
T
means with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
satisfying the condition
3.37

are bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.

The proof of part a) is complete.

To prove part b) of Theorem 2 we use the martingale, defined by (
3.28
) where
\alpha_{k}
satisfy conditions (
3.25
), (
3.26
) and (
3.27
). It is easy to show that for every non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
it automatically holds that

q_{M_{\alpha_{k}+1}}/Q_{M_{\alpha_{k}+2}}\geq c/M_{\alpha_{k}}.

According to (
3.34
), (
3.35
) and (
3.36
) we can conclude that

\left|T_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}f\right|\geq\left|II\right|-\left|I\right|\geq\frac{M_{\alpha_{k}}^{1/p-2}}{8\alpha_{k}}.

Analogously to (
3.35
) we then get that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|T_{M_{\alpha_{k}}+2}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}=\infty.

The proof is complete.

A number of special cases of our results are of particular interest and give both well-known and new information. We just give the following examples of such
T
means with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}:

Corollary 3.16

The maximal operator
B^{\alpha,\beta,*}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}
but is not bounded from
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p},

when
0<p<1/2.

Proof:
Since
B^{\alpha,\beta,*}
are the
T
means with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
then the proof of this corollary is direct consequence of Theorem
3.15
.

Corollary 3.17

Let
f\in L_{1}
and
T_{n}
be the
T
means with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
and satisfying condition (
3.37
). Then

T_{n}f\rightarrow f,\text{ \ \ a.e., \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof of Corollary
3.17
.
According to Theorem
3.15
and Lemma and
1.5
we can conclude that
T^{\ast}
has weak type-(1,1) and by well-known density argument due to Marcinkiewicz and Zygmund
[
192
]
(see Lemma
1.1
) we also have
T_{n}f\rightarrow f,
a.e.. Which follows proof of Corollary
3.17
.

Corollary 3.18

Let
f\in L_{1}
. Then
\ B_{n}^{\alpha,\beta}f\rightarrow f,\text{ \ \ \ a.e., \ \ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof:
Since
B^{\alpha,\beta,*}
are the
T
means with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
then the proof of this corollary is direct consequence of Corollary
3.17
.

Theorem 3.19

Let
0<p\leq 1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers. Then the maximal operator

\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Proof:

Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be non-increasing. By combining (
3.15
) and (
3.17
) we get that

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}f

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}q_{j}S_{j}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|+q_{n-1}(n-1)\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|}{{\left(j+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(j+1\right)}}+\frac{q_{n-1}(n-1)\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right)j+q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}:=\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f,

so that

\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}f\leq\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f.

(3.39)

If we apply (
3.39
), according (see Tephnadze
[
139
,
140
]
) that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}
for
p\leq 1/2
we can conclude that the maximal operators
\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}
of
T
means with non-increasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
are bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}
.

The proof of theorem is complete.

and

is bounded from the Hardy space

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}

Theorem 3.23

Let
0<p\leq 1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing
numbers, satisfying the condition

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ \ \ as \ \ }\ n\rightarrow\infty.

(3.40)

Then the maximal operator

\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Proof:

Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be non-decreasing satisfying the condition (
3.40
). By combining (
3.15
) and (
3.17
) we get that

\displaystyle\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}q_{j}S_{j}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|+q_{n-1}(n-1)\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|q_{j}-q_{j+1}\right|j\left|\sigma_{j}f\right|}{{\left(j+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(j+1\right)}}+\frac{q_{n-1}(n-1)\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\left(\overset{n-2}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(q_{j+1}-q_{j}\right)j+q_{n-1}(n-1)\right)\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2q_{n-1}(n-1)-Q_{n}}{Q_{n}}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

so that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|T_{n}f\right|}{{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}}\leq\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}.

(3.41)

If we apply (
3.41
), according (see Tephnadze
[
139
,
140
]
) that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}
for
p\leq 1/2
we can conclude that the maximal operators
\widetilde{T}_{p}^{\ast}
of
T
means with non-decreasing sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\},
are bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}
.

Corollary 3.24

Let
0<p\leq 1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-decreasing numbers, such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}q_{n}<c<\infty.

(3.42)

Then all such
T
means are bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Proof:

By using (
3.42
) we get

\frac{q_{n-1}}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{Q_{n}}\leq\frac{c}{q_{0}n}=\frac{c_{1}}{n}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ as }n\rightarrow 0,

It follows that condition (
3.40
) is satisfied and for such
T
means is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Remark 3.25

Since (see Tephnadze
[
139
,
140
]
) the maximal operator

\widetilde{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the martingale Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}
and the rate of denominator
\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2\left[1/2+p\right]}
is in the sense sharp and Fejer means is example of
T
means of as non-increasing as non-decreasing sequences we obtain that this weights are also sharp in Theorems
3.19
and
3.23
.

3.4
Strong convergence of
T
means with respect to Vilenkin systems on the martingale Hardy spaces

Theorem 3.26

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing numbers. Then there exists an absolute
constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that the inequality

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

holds.

b)Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing
numbers, satisfying the condition

\frac{1}{Q_{n}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),\text{ \ \ as \ \ }\ n\rightarrow\infty.

(3.43)

Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that the inequality

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

(3.44)

holds.

Proof:
Let the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be non-increasing. By Lemma
1.3
(see also (
1.4
)), the proof of part a) will be complete, if we show that

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{m}a\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p},

(3.45)

for every
p
-atom
a,
with support
\ I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.

We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=T_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore, we can suppose
that
n>M_{N}
.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
T_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(boundedness follows from Lemma
3.2
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{1/p}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}}{M_{N}}\leq c<\infty,\text{ \ }0<p<1/2.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-2p}}

(3.46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c<\infty.

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{N}}a\left(t\right)F_{n}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)\right|

(3.47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{N}}a\left(t\right)\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{N}^{1/p}\int_{I_{N}}\left|\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

Let
T_{n}
be
T
means, with non-decreasing coefficients
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
and
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l\leq N.
Then, in the view of Lemma
2.5
we get that

\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq cM_{l}M_{k}M_{N}^{1/p-2},\text{ for }0<p\leq 1/2.

(3.48)

Let
0<p<1/2.
By using (
1.1
), (
3.47
), (
3.48
) we find that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,\text{ }j\in\{l+1,\dots,N-1\}}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

(3.49)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\left(M_{l}M_{k}\right)^{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{p}M_{N}^{1-p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cM_{N}^{1-2p}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{\left(M_{l}M_{k}\right)^{p}}{M_{l}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}\leq cM_{N}^{1-2p}.

Moreover, according to (
3.49
), we get that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{m^{2-2p}}\leq\overset{\infty}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{cM_{N}^{1-2p}}{m^{2-2p}}<c<\infty,\text{
\ }\left(0<p<1/2\right).

The proof of part a) is complete.

Let
p=1/2
and
T_{n}
be
T
means, with non-increasing coefficients
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
, satisfying condition (
3.43
). By Lemma
1.3
, the proof of part b) will be complete, if we show that

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{m}a\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}}{m}\leq c_{p},

(3.50)

for every
p
-atom
a,
with support
\ I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.

We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
S_{n}\left(a\right)=T_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq M_{N}
. Therefore, we can suppose
that
n>M_{N}
.

Let
x\in I_{N}.
Since
T_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}
(boundedness follows from Lemma
3.2
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq M_{N}^{2}
we obtain that

\int_{I_{N}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu\leq\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{1/2}}{M_{N}}\leq c<\infty,\text{ \ }0<p\leq 1/2.

Hence,

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{N}}\left|T_{m}a\right|^{1/2}d\mu}{m}\leq\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m}\leq c<\infty.

(3.51)

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{I_{N}}a\left(t\right)\frac{1}{Q_{n}}\overset{n}{\underset{j=M_{N}}{\sum}}q_{j}D_{j}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)\right|

(3.52)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{N}^{2}\int_{I_{N}}\left|F_{m}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,l},\,0\leq k<l<N.
Then, in the view of Lemma
3.6

we get that

\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}M_{N}}{m}.

(3.53)

Let
x\in I_{N}^{k,N}.
Then, according to Lemma
3.6
we obtain that

\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq cM_{k}M_{N}.

(3.54)

By combining (
1.1
), (
3.52
), (
3.53
) and (
3.54
) we
obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dotsm m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\frac{\left(M_{l}M_{k}\right)^{1/2}M_{N}^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{1/2}M_{N}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{N}^{1/2}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{\left(M_{l}M_{k}\right)^{1/2}}{m^{1/2}M_{l}}+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1/2}}{M_{N}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{N}^{1/2}N}{m^{1/2}}+c.

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|T_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)}{m}

(3.55)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{cM_{N}^{1/2}N}{m^{3/2}}+\frac{c}{m}\right)<c<\infty.

The proof of part b) is completed by just combining (
3.51
) and (
3.55
).

such that the following inequality holds:

Corollary 3.30

Let
0<p\leq 1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that the following inequality holds:

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|R_{k}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Theorem 3.31

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-decreasing numbers. Then there exists an absolute
constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that the inequality

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

holds.

b)Let
f\in H_{1/2}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of non-increasing
numbers, satisfying the condition (
3.13
).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that the inequality

\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|T_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}}{k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}}^{1/2}

(3.56)

holds.

Proof:
[Proof of Theorem
3.31
]
If we use Lemmas
3.9
and
3.10
and follows analogical steps of Theorem
3.26
we immediately get the proof of Theorem
3.31
. So, we leave out the details.

Corollary 3.32

Let
0<p\leq 1/2,

f\in H_{p}
and
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be a sequence of
non-decreasing numbers, such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}q_{n}<c<\infty.

Then condition (
3.13
) is satisfied and for such
T
means there
exists an absolute constant
c,
such that the inequality (
3.56
) holds.

We have already considered the case when the sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}
is
bounded. Now, we consider some Nörlund means, which are generated by a
unbounded sequence
\{q_{k}:k\geq 0\}.

Corollary 3.33

Let
0<p\leq 1/2
and
f\in H_{p}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that the following inequality holds:

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|B^{\alpha,\beta}_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

4
Reisz and Nörlund logarithmoic means means on
H_{p}
spaces

4.1
Introduction

Riesz logarithmic means with respect to the trigonometric system was studied by a lot of authors. We mention, for instance, the paper by Szasz
[
135
]
and Yabuta
[
181
]
.
These means with respect to the Walsh and Vilenkin systems were investigated
by Simon
[
119
]
and Gàt
[
38
]
. Blahota and Gát
[
19
]

considered norm summability of Nörlund logarithmic means and showed that
Riesz logarithmic means
R_{n}
have better approximation properties on some
unbounded Vilenkin groups than the Fejér means. Moreover, in
[
147
]

it was proved that the maximal operator of Riesz means is bounded from the
Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}
for
p>1/2
but not
when
0<p\leq 1/2.
Strong convergence theorems and boundedness of weighted maximal operators of Riesz logarithmic means was considered in Lukkassen, Persson, Tutberidze, Tephnadze
[
76
]
and Tephnadze
[
147
]
.

In
[
148
]
Tephnadze proved that the maximal operator of Riesz logarithmic means
R^{\ast}
is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
weak-L_{1/2}.
Moreover, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},
where
0<p\leq 1/2
such that

\left\|R^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}=+\infty.

In
[
147
]
Tephnadze proved that for any
0<p<1/2,
the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{R}_{p}^{\ast}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\log n\left|R_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
L_{p}.

Moreover, for
0<p<1/2
and non-decreasing function
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
satisfying the condition

\frac{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}}{\log\left(n+1\right)\varphi\left(n\right)}=\infty,

(4.1)

the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|R_{n}f\right|}{\varphi\left(n\right)}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the space
weak-L_{p}.

In the case
p=1/2
he also proved that the maximal operator

\widetilde{R}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|R_{n}f\right|}{\log\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
L_{1/2}.

Moreover, for any non-decreasing function
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log\left(n+1\right)}{\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|R_{n}f\right|}{\varphi\left(n\right)}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1/2}
to the space
L_{1/2}.

In this thesis (see also
[
76
]
) we also proved that if
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G_{m}),
there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that the inequality

\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\frac{\log^{p}n\left\|R_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}}{n^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}

holds, where
R_{n}f
denotes the
n
-th Reisz logarithmic mean with respect to the Vilenkin-Fourier series of
f.

Móricz and Siddiqi
[
82
]
investigate the approximation properties of
some special Nörlund means of Walsh-Fourier series of
L_{p}
functions in
norm. The case when
\left\{q_{k}=1/k:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
was
excluded, since the methods of Móricz and Siddiqi are not applicable to Nörlund logarithmic means.
Fridli, Manchanda and Siddiqi
[
34
]
improved and extended results of Móricz and Siddiqi
[
82
]
to dyadic homogeneous Banach spaces and Martingale Hardy spaces.
In
[
39
]
Gát and Goginava proved some
convergence and divergence properties of the Nörlund logarithmic means of
functions in the class of continuous functions and in the Lebesgue space
L_{1}.
In particular, they gave a negative answer to the question of Móricz
and Siddiqi
[
82
]
. Gát and Goginava
[
40
]
proved that for each
measurable function satisfying

\phi\left(u\right)=o\left(u\log^{1/2}u\right),\ as\ u\rightarrow\infty,

there exists an integrable function
f
such that

\int_{G_{m}}\phi\left(\left|f\left(x\right)\right|\right)d\mu\,\left(x\right)<\infty

and that there exists a set with positive measure such that the
Walsh-logarithmic means of the function diverges on this set.
It follows that that weak-(1,1) type inequality does not hold for the maximal operator of Nörlund logarithmic means:

\displaystyle L^{\ast}f

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|L_{n}f\right|

On the other hand, there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
such that

\left\|L^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{p},\text{ \ when \ }f\in L_{p},\text{ \ }p>1.

If we consider the following restricted maximal operator

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}_{\#}^{\ast}f

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|L_{M_{n}}f\right|,\ \ \ (M_{k}:=m_{0}...m_{k-1},\ \ \ k=0,1...)

then

\displaystyle\lambda\mu\left\{\widetilde{L}_{\#}^{\ast}f>\lambda\right\}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{1},\ \ \ f\in L_{1}(G_{m}),\ \ \lambda>0.

Hence, if
f\in L_{1}(G_{m})
then

L_{M_{n}}f\to f,\ \ \text{a.e. on }\ \ G_{m}.

In this thesis we prove that if
f\in L_{1}(G_{m})
then
L_{M_{n}}f(x)\to f(x)
for all Lebesgue points.

In
[
141
]
it was proved that there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p},

(0<p\leq 1),
such that the maximal operator of Nörlund logarithmic means
L^{\ast}
is not bounded in the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.
In particular, it was proved that there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}
such that

\left\|L^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}=+\infty.

Boundedness of weighted maximal operators of Nörlund logarithmic means was considered Persson, Tephnadze, Wall
[
105
]
. In particular, maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{L}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\log\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space
L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right).

Moreover, if
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\log\left(n+1\right)}{\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

(4.2)

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}\left(G_{m}\right),
such that the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\varphi\left(n\right)}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{1}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the
Lebesgue space
L_{1}\left(G_{m}\right).

In Tephnadze and Tutberidze
[
167
]
was proved the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{L}_{p}^{*}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space
L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).

We also proved that for
0<p<1
and a non-decreasing function
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{1/p-1}}{\log n\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right),
such that the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\varphi\left(n+1\right)}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space
L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).

In this paper also state the following open problem:

Open Problem.
For any
0<p<1
let find non-decreasing function
\Theta:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
suth that the following maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{L}_{p}^{*}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\Theta\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
and the rate of
\Theta:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
is sharp, that is, for any non-decreasing function

\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\Theta\left(n\right)}{\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right),
such that the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\varphi\left(n+1\right)}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space

L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).

According to Theorems above we can conclude that there exist absolute constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
such that

\frac{C_{1}n^{1/p-1}}{\log(n+1)}\leq\Theta\left(n\right)\leq C_{2}n^{1/p-1}.

Later on, Memic generalized result of Tephnadze and Tutberidze
[
167
]
and proved that the maximal operator

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\log n\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space
L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).

Sharpness of this result immediately follows negative result of Tephnadze and Tutberidze
[
167
]
, which is already stated above.

4.2
Auxiliary lemmas

We need the following lemma of independent interest:

Lemma 4.1

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

Y_{n}=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\frac{K_{j}}{j+1}+\frac{K_{n}}{l_{n}}.

(4.3)

Moreover,

\|Y_{n}\|_{1}<c<\infty.

(4.4)

Proof:
To rewrite the kernels of the Riesz
logarithmic means as in equality (
4.3
) we have to just use Abel transformation. On the other hand, equality (
4.4
) immediately follows equality (
4.3
) and (
1.12
).

To prove our main results we need the following lemma, which is proved in Tephnadze
[
144
]
. Here we also give some short proof.

Lemma 4.2

Let
x\in I_{N}\left(x_{k}e_{k}+x_{l}e_{l}\right),

\ 1\leq x_{k}\leq m_{k}-1,

1\leq x_{l}\leq m_{l}-1,

\ k=0,...,N-2,

l=k+1,...,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\frac{\left|K_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|}{j+1}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{M_{N}^{2}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}\left(x_{k}e_{k}\right),

1\leq x_{k}\leq m_{k}-1,

\ k=0,...,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\frac{\left|K_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|}{j+1}d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}l_{n}.

proof:
Let
x\in I_{N}\left(x_{k}e_{k}+x_{l}e_{l}\right),

\ 1\leq x_{k}\leq m_{k}-1,

1\leq x_{l}\leq m_{l}-1,

\ k=0,...,N-2,

l=k+1,...,N-1.
By using Lemma
2.4
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\frac{\left|K_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|}{j+1}d\mu\left(t\right)

(4.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{\left(j+1\right)jM_{N}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{M_{N}}\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\left(\frac{1}{j}-\frac{1}{j+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}M_{l}}{M_{N}^{2}}.

Let
x\in I_{N}\left(x_{k}e_{k}\right),

1\leq x_{k}\leq m_{k}-1,

\ k=0,...,N-1.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\frac{\left|K_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|}{j+1}d\mu\left(t\right)

(4.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\frac{cM_{k}}{\left(j+1\right)M_{N}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{k}}{M_{N}}l_{n}.

Combining (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) we complete the proof of Lemma.

Next we study some special consequences of kernels of Nörlund logarithmic means:

Lemma 4.3

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

\displaystyle P_{M_{n}}(x)=D_{M_{n}}(x)-\psi_{M_{n}-1}(x)\overline{Y}_{M_{n}}(x)

(4.7)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\|P_{M_{n}}(x)\|_{1}<c<\infty.

(4.8)

Proof:
By using
1.4
we get that

\displaystyle P_{M_{n}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{M_{n}}}\overset{M_{n}}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{D_{k}(x)}{M_{n}-k}=\frac{1}{Q_{M_{n}}}\overset{M_{n}-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{D_{M_{n}-k}(x)}{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{Q_{M_{n}}}\overset{M_{n}-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\left(D_{M_{n}}(x)-\psi_{M_{n}-1}(x)\overline{D}_{k}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{M_{n}}(x)-\psi_{M_{n}-1}(x)\overline{Y}_{M_{n}}(x).

which compete proof of identity (
4.7
).

On the other hand, if we combine (
1.8
) and (
1.24
) we also get proof of (
4.8
). The proof is complete.

The proof of next Lemma can be found in Tephnadze
[
143
]
:

Lemma 4.4

Let
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},\ \ s=0,...,N-1.
Then

\int_{I_{N}}\left|P_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{cM_{s}}{M_{N}},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof:
The proof is direct consequence of Lemma
2.1
. So, we leave out the details.

4.3
strong convergence of Reisz means means with respect to Vilenkin systems on the martingale Hardy spaces

Our first main result reads:

Theorem 4.5

Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G_{m}).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that the inequality

\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\frac{\log^{p}n\left\|R_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G_{m})}^{p}}{n^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G_{m})}^{p}

(4.9)

holds, where
Rf
denotes the
n
-th Reisz logarithmic mean with respect to the Vilenkin-Fourier series of
f.

Proof:
According to (
4.4
) in Lemma
4.1
we get that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{G_{m}}\left|Y_{n}a\right|d\mu\leq c<\infty.

and it follows that
R_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}
to
L_{\infty}.

By Proposition
1.4
, the proof of theorem will be complete, if we show that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=1}{\sum}}\frac{\log^{p}n\int\limits_{\overset{-}{I}}\left|R_{n}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{n^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}<\infty,\ \ \text{for}\ \ 0<p<1/2,

(4.10)

for every
p
-atom
a,
where
I
denotes the support of the atom.

Let
a
be an arbitrary p-atom with support
I
and
\mu\left(I\right)=M_{N}^{-1}.
We may assume that
I=I_{N}.
It is easy to see that
R_{n}a=\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq M_{N}
.
Therefore we suppose that
n>M_{N}.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq cM_{N}^{2}
if we apply (
4.3
) in Lemma
4.1
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left|R_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|

(4.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|Y_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu(t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}}\left|Y_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{N}^{1/p}}{l_{n}}\underset{I_{N}}{\int}\underset{j=M_{N}+1}{\overset{n-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|K_{j}\left(x-t\right)\right|}{j+1}d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{N}^{1/p}}{l_{n}}\int_{I_{N}}\left|K_{n}\left(x-t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{N}\left(x_{k}e_{k}+x_{l}e_{l}\right),

\ 1\leq x_{k}\leq m_{k}-1,

1\leq x_{l}\leq m_{l}-1,

k=0,...,N-2,

l=k+1,...,N-1.
From
Lemma
4.2
it follows that

\left|R_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{cM_{l}M_{k}M_{N}^{1/p-2}}{\log(n+1)}.

(4.12)

Let
x\in I_{N}\left(x_{k}e_{k}\right),

1\leq x_{k}\leq m_{k}-1,

k=0,...,N-1.
Applying Lemma
4.2
we can conclude that

\left|R_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq{M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{k}}.

(4.13)

By combining (
1.1
) and (
4.11
-
4.13
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|R_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

(4.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\sum\limits_{x_{j}=0,j\in\{l+1,...,N-1}^{m_{j-1}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,l}}\left|R_{n}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{N}^{k,N}}\left|R_{n}a\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{m_{l+1}\dots m_{N-1}}{M_{N}}\frac{\left(M_{l}M_{k}\right)^{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}}{{\log}^{p}(n+1)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{N}}M_{k}^{p}M_{N}^{1-p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{N}^{1-2p}}{{\log^{p}(n+1)}}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{\left(M_{l}M_{k}\right)^{p}}{M_{l}}+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{N}^{1-2p}}{\log^{p}(n+1)}\overset{N-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{M_{k}^{1-2p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{1-p}}{M_{l}^{1-p}}+\overset{N-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{M_{k}^{p}}{M_{N}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{cM_{N}^{1-2p}}{\log^{p}(n+1)}+c_{p}.

It is easy to see that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{n^{2-2p}}\leq\frac{c}{M_{N}^{1-2p}},\ \ \text{ for }\ \ 0<p<1/2.

(4.15)

By combining (
4.14
) and (
4.15
) we get that

\displaystyle\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\log^{p}n\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|R_{n}a\right|^{p}d\mu}{n^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\left(\frac{c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}}{n^{2-p}}+\frac{c_{p}}{n^{2-p}}\right)+c_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{N}^{1-2p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{n^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{\infty}{\underset{n=M_{N}+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{n^{2-p}}+c_{p}\leq C_{p}<\infty.

It means that (
4.10
) holds true and the proof is complete.

Our next main result shows in particular that the inequality in Theorem
4.5
is in a special sense sharp at least in the case of Walsh-Fourier series.

Theorem 4.6

Let
0<p<1/2
and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be any non-decreasing function, satisfying the condition

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\Phi\left(n\right)=+\infty.

(4.16)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}\left(G_{2}\right)
such that

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\log^{p}n\left\|R^{w}_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}\Phi\left(n\right)}{n^{2-2p}}=\infty,

(4.17)

where
R^{w}_{n}f
denotes the
n
-th Reisz logarithmic means with respect to Walsh-Fourier series of

f.

Proof:

It is evident that if we assume that
\Phi\left(n\right)\geq cn,
where
c
is some positive constant then

\frac{\log^{p}n\Phi\left(n\right)}{n^{2-2p}}\geq n^{1-2p}\log^{p}n\rightarrow\infty,\ \text{as}\ n\rightarrow\infty,

and also (
4.17
) holds. So, without lost the generality we may assume that there exists an increasing sequence of positive integers

\left\{\alpha^{\prime}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
such that

\Phi\left(\alpha^{\prime}_{k}\right)=o(\alpha^{\prime}_{k}),\ \text{as}\ k\rightarrow\infty.

(4.18)

Let
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\subseteq\left\{\alpha^{\prime}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be an increasing sequence of positive integers such that
\alpha_{0}\geq 2
and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}<\infty,

(4.19)

\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}/p}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}\leq\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k-1}/p+1}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k-1}})},

(4.20)

\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k-1}/p+1}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k-1}})}\leq\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}.

(4.21)

We note that under condition (
4.18
) we can conclude that

\frac{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}/p}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}\geq{\left(\frac{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}{\Phi(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}\right)}^{1/2p}\rightarrow\infty,\ \text{as}\ \eta\rightarrow\infty

and it immediately follows that such an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\},
which satisfies conditions (
4.19
)-(
4.21
), can be constructed.

Let

f^{\left(n\right)}\left(x\right):=\sum_{\left\{k;\text{ }2\alpha_{k}<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k},

where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{1}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}

and

a_{k}={2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}\left(D_{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}}-D_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\right).

From (
4.19
) and Lemma
1.3
we can conclude that
f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}(G_{2}).

It is easy to show that

\widehat{f}^{w}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})},\,\,\text{ if \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{2^{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N},\\
0,\text{ \thinspace\thinspace\thinspace if \thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{2^{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

(4.22)

For
n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}2^{n_{i}},

n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{s}
we denote

\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:=\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\text{ }n=2^{0}+2^{2}+\sum_{i=3}^{s_{n}}2^{n_{i}}\right\}.

Let
2^{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq 2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1
and
j\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}.
Then

\displaystyle R^{w}_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\sum_{n=1}^{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-1}\frac{S_{n}f}{n}+\frac{1}{l_{j}}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}}}^{j}\frac{S_{n}f}{n}:=I+II.

(4.23)

Let
n<2^{2\alpha_{k}}.
Then from (
4.20
), (
4.21
) and (
4.22
) we have that

\displaystyle\left|S^{w}_{n}f\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\sum_{v=2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\left|\widehat{f}^{w}(v)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\sum_{v=2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}/p}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k-1}/p+1}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k-1}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\left|I\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\underset{n=1}{\overset{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left|S^{w}_{n}f\left(x\right)\right|}{n}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}}\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\sum_{n=1}^{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-1}\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}.

Let
2^{2\alpha_{k}}\leq n\leq 2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1.
Then we have the
following

\displaystyle S^{w}_{n}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\sum_{v=2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\widehat{f}^{w}(v)w_{v}+\sum_{v=2^{2\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\widehat{f}^{w}(v)w_{v}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{{2\alpha_{\eta}}\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}\left(D^{w}_{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}-D^{w}_{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{{2\alpha_{k}}\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(D^{w}_{n}-D^{w}_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\right).

This gives that

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\underset{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}}}{\overset{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}}{\sum}}\ \frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}})}\left(D^{w}_{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}-D^{w}_{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}\right)\right)

(4.25)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}}}^{j}\frac{\left(D^{w}_{n}-D^{w}_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\right)}{n}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle II_{1}+II_{2}.

Let
x\in I_{2}(e_{0}+e_{1})\in I_{0}\backslash I_{1}.
According to
(
1.38
) and (
1.39
) we conclude that

D^{w}_{n}\left(x\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}w_{n},&\,\text{if\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}n\ \ \text{is odd
number,}\\
0,&\text{if}\,\,n\ \ \text{is even number.}\end{array}\right.

Since
\alpha_{0}\geq 2,\ \ k\in\mathbb{N}
we obtain that
2\alpha_{k}\geq 4,
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and if we apply (
1.38
) we get that

II_{1}=0

(4.26)

and

\displaystyle II_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-1}}^{(j-1)/2}\frac{w_{2n+1}}{2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}r_{1}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-1}}^{(j-1)/2}\frac{w_{2n}}{2n+1}.

Let
x\in I_{2}(e_{0}+e_{1}).
Then, by the definition of Walsh functions, we get that

w_{4n+2}=r_{1}w_{4n}=-w_{4n}

and

\displaystyle\left|II_{2}\right|=\frac{1}{l_{j}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left|\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-1}}^{(j-1)/2}\frac{w_{2n}}{2n+1}\right|

(4.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left|\frac{w_{j-1}}{j}+\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\left(\frac{w_{4n-4}}{4n-3}+\frac{w_{4n-2}}{4n-1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{j}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left|\frac{w_{j-1}}{j}+\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\left(\frac{w_{4n-4}}{4n-3}-\frac{w_{4n-2}}{4n-1}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{\log({2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}})}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\left|\frac{w_{j-1}}{j}\right|-\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\left|{w_{4n-4}}\right|\left(\frac{1}{4n-3}-\frac{1}{4n-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{1}{j}-\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\left(\frac{1}{4n-3}-\frac{1}{4n-1}\right)\right).

Hence, by simple calculation we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\left(\frac{1}{4n-3}-\frac{1}{4n-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\frac{2}{(4n-3)(4n-1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\frac{2}{(4n-4)(4n-2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\frac{1}{(2n-2)(2n-1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\frac{1}{(2n-2)(2n-2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\frac{1}{(n-1)(n-1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\sum_{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\frac{1}{(n-1)(n-2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\sum_{l=2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}+1}^{(j-1)/4}\left(\frac{1}{n-2}-\frac{1}{n-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}-1}-\frac{4}{j-5}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}-1}-\frac{4}{j}\right).

Since
2^{2\alpha_{k}}\leq j\leq 2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1,
where
\alpha_{k}\geq 2,
we obtain that

\frac{2}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-4}\leq\frac{2}{2^{4}-4}=\frac{1}{6}

and

\displaystyle\left|II_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{1}{j}-\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}-2}-1}-\frac{4}{j}\right)\right)

(4.28)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{3}{2j}-\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-8}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{3}{4}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-4}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-4}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}-\frac{2}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}(2^{2\alpha_{k}}-4)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\left(\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}-\frac{1}{6}\frac{1}{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{48\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{64\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}.

By combining (
4.2
), (
4.23
)-(
4.28
) for
\in I_{2}(e_{0}+e_{1})
and
0<p<1/2
we find that

\displaystyle\left|R^{w}_{j}f\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|II_{2}\right|-\left|II_{1}\right|-\left|I\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{64\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}-\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}.

and

\displaystyle\left\|R^{w}_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G_{2})}^{p}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}^{p}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1-2p)}}{\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\mu\left\{x\in G_{2}:\left|R^{w}_{j}f\right|\geq\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}^{p}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1-2p)}}{\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\mu\left\{x\in I_{2}(e_{0}+e_{1}):\left|R^{w}_{j}f\right|\geq\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-2)}}{\Phi^{1/2p}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{128\alpha_{k}^{p}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1-2p)}}{\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}(\mu\left(x\in I_{2}(e_{0}+e_{1})\right))

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{1}{516\alpha_{k}^{p}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1-2p)}}{\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\underset{j=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|R^{w}_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G_{2})}^{p}\log^{p}{(j)}\Phi(j)}{j^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:\text{ }2^{2\alpha_{k}}<j\leq 2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}}{\sum}\frac{\left\|R^{w}_{j}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}}^{p}\log^{p}{(j)}\Phi(j)}{j^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{\alpha_{k}^{p}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1-2p)}}{\Phi^{p/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k})}}\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:\text{ }2^{2\alpha_{k}}<j\leq 2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}}{\sum}\frac{\log^{p}{(j)}\Phi(j)}{j^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\Phi(2^{2\alpha_{k}})\log^{p}{(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}}{\alpha_{k}^{p}}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}(1-2p)}}{\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})}\underset{\left\{j\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:\text{ }2^{2\alpha_{k}}<j\leq 2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}}{\sum}\frac{1}{j^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\Phi^{1/2}(2^{2\alpha_{k}})\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

Theorem 4.7

Let
p\geq 1
and
f\in L_{p}
. Then

\|R_{n}f-f\|_{p}\to 0\ \ \text{as}\ \ n\to\infty

(4.30)

for all
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
. Moreover,

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}R_{n}f(x)=f(x)

for all Vilenkin-Lebesgue points of
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
.

Proof:
By using Abel transformation we get

R_{n}=\frac{1}{l_{n}}\overset{n-1}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\frac{\sigma_{j}}{j+1}+\frac{\sigma_{n}}{l_{n}}.

(4.31)

Since (for details see Yano
[
182
]
)

\|\sigma_{n}f-f\|_{p}\to 0\ \ \text{as}\ \ n\to\infty

if we apply (
4.31
) we immediately get (
4.30
).

On the other hand, since (for details see
[
53
]
)

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\sigma_{n}f(x)=f(x)

for all Vilenkin-Lebesgue points of
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
, if we invoke again equality (
4.31
) we also get

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}R_{n}f(x)=f(x)

for all Vilenkin-Lebesgue points of
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
.

4.4
Nörlund logarithmic means of Vilenkin-Fourier series on Martingale Hardy spaces

Theorem 4.8

a) Let
0<p<1.
Then the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{L}_{p}^{*}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space
L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).

b) Let
0<p<1
and
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be a
non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{1/p-1}}{\log n\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty.

(4.32)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right),
such that the maximal operator

\text{ }\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\varphi\left(n+1\right)}

is not bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right)
to the space

L_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).

Proof:
Since

\displaystyle\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sup}\left|S_{k}f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{1\leq k\leq n}{\sup}\frac{\left|S_{k}f\right|}{\left(k+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

and

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\leq\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

(4.33)

On the other hand, Tephnadze
[
144
]
(see also
[
146
]
and
[
149
]
) proved that for every
0<p<1,
the maximal operator

\widetilde{S}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}

is bounded from the Hardy space
H_{p}
to the Lebesgue space
L_{p}.

Hence, according to inequality (
4.33
) we can conclude that

\left\|\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|L_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\right\|_{p}\leq\left\|\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}}\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}.

Now, prove part b) of the Theorem. Let

f_{n_{k}}=D_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}}.

It is evident

\widehat{f}_{n_{k}}\left(i\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{ }1,\qquad\text{ if }\qquad i=M_{2n_{k}},...,M_{2n_{k}+1}-1,\\
\text{ }0,\qquad\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(4.34)

Then we can write

S_{i}f_{n_{k}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}D_{i}-D_{M_{2n_{k}}},\quad\text{ if }\quad i=M_{2n_{k}}+1,...,M_{2n_{k}+1}-1,\\
f_{n_{k}},\qquad\qquad\quad\text{ if }\quad i\geq M_{2n_{k}+1},\\
0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(4.35)

From (
1.5
) we get

\displaystyle\left\|f_{n_{k}}\right\|_{H_{p}}

(4.36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}S_{M_{n}}f_{n_{k}}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|D_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|D_{M_{2n_{k}+1}}\right\|_{p}+\left\|D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle cM_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1-1/p}<c<\infty.

Let
0<p<1
and
\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be an increasing sequence of the positive integers such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\lambda_{k}^{1/p-1}}{\varphi\left(\lambda_{k}\right)}=\infty.

Let
\ \left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
such that

\displaystyle\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+2\right)^{1/p-1}}{\log{\left(M_{2n_{k}}+2\right)}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}\geq c\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\lambda_{k}^{1/p-1}}{\varphi\left(\lambda_{k}\right)}=\infty.

According to (
4.35
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{L_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}f_{n_{k}}}{\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left|D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+1}-D_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right|}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+1}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\psi_{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}}\right|}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+1}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+1}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}.

Hence,

\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\left|L_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}f_{n_{k}}\right|\geq\frac{1}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}\right\}=\mu\left(G_{m}\right)=1.

(4.37)

By combining (
4.36
) and (
4.37
) we get that

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}\left(\mu\left\{x\in G_{m}:\hskip 10.00002pt\left|L_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}f_{n_{k}}\right|\geq\frac{1}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}\right\}\right)^{1/p}}{\left\|f_{n_{k}}\right\|_{p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}^{1/p-1}}{l_{M_{2n_{k}}+2}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\left(M_{{}_{2n_{k}}}+2\right)^{1/p-1}}{\log{\left(M_{2n_{k}}+2\right)}\varphi\left(M_{2n_{k}+2}\right)}\rightarrow\infty,\text{\hskip 10.00002ptas \hskip 10.00002pt}k\rightarrow\infty.

Theorem is proved.

Corollary 4.9

Let
0<p<1\
and
f\in H_{p}\left(G_{m}\right).
Then
there is an absolute constant
c_{p},
depends only
p,
such that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|L_{k}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}^{p}.

Proof:
If we apply Lemma
1.4
we only have to prove that

\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|L_{k}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

for every
p
atom
a
, with support
I_{N}
and
\mu\left(I_{N}\right)=M_{N}^{-1}
. Since
S_{k}a=0,
for
k\leq M_{N},
we get that
L_{n}a=0,
for
n\leq M_{N}.
So, we may assume that
n>M_{N}.

Let
x\in\overline{I_{N}}
and
0<p<1.
Applying (
1.1
) and Lemma
4.4
we have that

\displaystyle\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|L_{k}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{k^{2-p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\int_{\overline{I_{N}}}\left|\frac{L_{k}a}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\frac{L_{k}a}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|\frac{M_{N}^{1/p-1}M_{s}}{k^{1/p-1}}\right|^{p}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}M_{s}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\overset{N-1}{\underset{s=0}{\sum}}M_{s}^{p-1}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c_{p}M_{N}^{1-p}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=M_{N}}{\sum}}\frac{1}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

Which complete the proof.

Theorem 4.10

Let
p\geq 1
and
f\in L_{p}
. Then

\|L_{M_{n}}f-f\|_{p}\to 0\ \ \text{as}\ \ n\to\infty

for all
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
. Moreover,

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}L_{M_{n}}f(x)=f(x)

for all Lebesgue points of
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
.

Proof:
To prove a.e convergence we use first identity in Lemma
4.3
to write that

\displaystyle L_{M_{n}}f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{G_{m}}{\int}f\left(t\right)P_{n}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underset{G_{m}}{\int}f\left(t\right)D_{M_{n}}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\underset{G_{m}}{\int}f\left(t\right)\psi_{M_{n}-1}(x-t)\overline{Y}_{M_{n}}(x-t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I-II.

By applying (
1.26
) we can conclude that

I=S_{M_{n}}f(x)\to f(x)

for all Lebesgue points of
f\in L_{p}(G_{m})
.
By using (
1.2
) we can conclude that

II=\psi_{M_{n}-1}(x)\underset{G_{m}}{\int}f\left(t\right)\overline{Y}_{M_{n}}(x-t)\overline{\psi}_{M_{n}-1}(t)d(t)

By combining
4.8
in Lemma
4.3
and
1.26
we find that

f\left(t\right)\overline{Y}_{M_{n}}(x-t)\in L_{p}\ \ \text{ where}\ \ p\geq 1\ \ \text{for any }\ \ x\in G_{m},

and
II
is Fourier coefficients of integrable function. According to Riemann-Lebesgue Lemma it vanish as
n\to\infty,

II\to 0\ \ \text{for any }\ \ x\in G_{m},\ \ n\to\infty.

The proof is complete.
